- Host first: with a replay client and a worker that waits 93 us (spinning or blocking), cold wake-ups cost 70 to 80 us only after about a millisecond idle; at the real inter-call gaps (76 to 150 us, from row wall time minus solver wall time) they cost at most 15 us. - One Modal A100 80GB PCIe job (owner's one-run waiver, second use today), 72 cells (null and CUDA worker x reply block/spin x worker spin 0/2000 us x gap 0/100/1000 us x 3 repeats); record `20261116T1532Z-ic-gpu-wake-probe-a100` (closed, measured). Preregistered rules: reproduction yes (U = 94.4 us), H1 (client wake) not supported (7.6 us against a 40 us bar), H2 (worker wake) not supported (at most 13.4 us), H3 as stated (88.7 us with both sides spinning). U is 80 to 108 us in all 12 CUDA cells. [pcc:unverified]
- The medians showed it: median round trip 97 to 128 us against a mean of 190 to 240 and a busy mean of about 92. Fitting the cumulative mean round trip printed every 500 calls (already in both arms of the earlier job's logs) to b + F/n gives residuals under 1 us: steady-state b = 107.9 us (24 unknowns) and 184.9 us (26) on main, 168.1 and 251.5 on the text-protocol arm, and a one-time F of 146 to 178 ms in both arms. [pcc:unverified]
- Report, figure, `fit_startup.py`, raw files and `research/ic_gpu_ipc_split_20261116/ERRATA.md` (the earlier files are pinned by their record and were not edited).


- The remainder in PR #4380 is a one-time start-up, not a per-call cost: the CUDA worker creates its context and buffers before it reads its first request, so the first call waits about 150 ms. Steady state, derived from the fits: 0.423 to 0.119 ms (72 percent less) at 24 unknowns and 0.566 to 0.199 ms (65 percent less) at 26, instead of the 59 and 56 percent reported from means; the steady-state call at 24 unknowns is fully accounted for (kernel 69, worker copies and reply 24, pipe floor 13.5, Rust pack and verify 10, remainder 1.5 us).
- The spin option stays (off by default) only so that the probe reproduces; the hypotheses it tested were not supported.
- No leaderboard or `benchmarks/README.md` row (wall time only; no validator report or matched rho).


- Hiding the 150 ms start-up: spawn the CUDA worker early so its initialisation overlaps other work (the first call currently waits for it). It matters for short `ic bench` rows (a 1006-call row took 0.19 s, so F is most of it) and not for long runs. Needs the first-call latency measured directly with and without an early spawn, which is another A100 run (waiver or token).
- In steady state the kernel is 58 to 72 percent of a call; further per-call gains are in the kernel, then the worker's copies and launch (24 us).
- The identity check of the probe is void for the CUDA worker (nondeterministic solution order; its checksum differs in all 36 CUDA runs); a sorted-set hash would fix it.


- My earlier reading ("about 0.085 ms per call that no phase timer locates") averaged away a start-up cost that the logs already showed (cumulative means falling from 440 to 197 us over 500 to 1878 calls); found by the medians in this probe. Corrected in the erratum and the report rather than by editing the pinned files.
- The probe's reply checksum cannot verify CUDA answers (see above); recorded as a metric and in the report.
